## A1-ay-waves

Recapitulated the full AY tranche (56 spec-file waves + PROGRESS/FINAL close rows) and spot-checked every load-bearing close-claim at HEAD (tranche/AY @ 86591cea, v3.10.1, NOT the 3.10.0 the docs name — a documented stale-registry recovery). HOWEVER: the BINDING R3 live audit (delivered 2026-06-10 15:27 MID-W-PUB1 against the shipped 3.10.1) CONTRADICTS several waves that PROGRESS/FINAL mark "live-verified" — and AY's own status legend states a real-device contradiction RE-OPENS the wave. The contradicted set (dock layers, blob render, the red hover register, dock-over-light) are OPEN-DEFECT regardless of clean source machinery: the rendered live result does not match the close. The named planned waves (W-LIQUID/W-BLOB-GLASS/W-AUR-T5/W-LIGHTHOUSE/W-SB2/W-SB3 + folded W-AUR1/W-BLOB1/W-FF1) are HONEST named-successor deferrals that fold into AZ. ## A5-precepts

Recapitulated the full precepts corpus (design-idioms.md, cross-repo-dev-resolution.md, TRANCHE-AND-WAVE-SPEC.md, AGENT_DISPATCH_TEMPLATE.md) + the CLAUDE.md binding invariants, and swept HEAD (3.10.1 @ 86591cea) for violations. 18 binding rules checked; 17 ADDRESSED (15 machine-locked by green proof:* gates I ran, 2 verified by direct grep + spec authority). ONE OPEN-DEFECT: the dialog `glass-top-layer::backdrop` uses `hsl(var(--background) / α)` where `--background` resolves to a complete `hsl()` color (`var(--neutral-0)` → `hsl(48 12% 98%)`), making it the invalid `hsl(hsl(...) / α)` double-wrap that CLAUDE.md forbids ("never paints") — the modal backdrop dim silently does not render, and scale-paper.css §297 MISLABELS it "the legitimate single-token alpha case." No proof gate covers this surface, so it is live and unguarded. The deliberate documented keeps (cn() hand-rolled / no tailwind-merge; surface-tint in-srgb vs glass-tint in-oklab; .focus-ring token-first divergence) are all intact, not drifted. The agent-git-clause is not violated by the W-PUB1.md git commands — that wave is explicitly framed as a USER-DOMAIN hinge ("no source edit by an agent"), so the documented commit/merge/push steps are orchestrator/user actions, not agent instructions.

## A6-standing-feedback

Swept HEAD (tranche/AY @ 86591cea, v3.10.1) for drift against the 10 standing-feedback house rules: no-backwards-compat, presets-in-consumers, no-grandiloquence, gestalt-over-incremental, tailwind-first, analyze-in-full, greenfield-no-meta, live-verify-delta, light-dark() inset-shadow trap, reka-binding verification. RESULT: NO shipping-artifact violations. The src/ migration-meta grep hits are all inline tranche-decision rationale (the house tranche vocabulary: "L invariant 4", "renamed from X → Y"), not the prohibited greenfield "ported from"/version-history meta. The AY/AZ doc hits for "no-backwards-compat"/"legacy"/"stunning" are the docs CITING the rule (adherence) or quoting the user's own "stunning bar" acceptance mandate — not editorializing or drift. Demo scoped-style hardcoded colors (auth-shell.vue, paper-backdrop-texture-system.vue) are legitimate demo-private token-override presets on the cascade (presets-in-consumers, correct). No light-dark() inset-shadow trap (the --shadow-cartoon-color tokens hold plain colors; no inset fragment composed inside light-dark()). No stale reka-bindings (:pressed/search-term/tag= absent; tag="div" is Vue TransitionGroup). No retired-name aliases on the public surface (GlassCarouselPager is a live primitive per CLAUDE.md). One LOW-severity stylistic observation: the AY wave-spec corpus uses spaced em-dashes ( — ) pervasively (3754 occurrences) against the MEMORY "em dashes without spaces" tic — but this is the consistent established house tranche-doc format (incl. the user-authored R3 audit doc), not a regression, so noted not flagged.

The single GlassCarousel* hit is GlassCarouselPager — a live shipping primitive (carousel/index.ts:8), not a retired alias.

PROPOSED freshness model (do not implement): replace git-ancestry-of-frozen-SHA with a CONTENT-HASH model — the DELTA declares `<!-- surface-hash: <sha256 of the concatenated surface-paths' bytes AT capture time> -->`; the gate recomputes the hash of the current surface-paths and compares. Fresh IFF the content is byte-identical to capture time, REGARDLESS of how many unrelated commits touched the file. This decouples freshness from commit-churn: a no-op formatting commit or an edit elsewhere in the same file no longer re-stales a capture whose RELEVANT pixels are unchanged. (A tighter variant scopes the hash to a function/selector range, but file-level is the cheap correct first step.)

## C2-morph-flicker

R3-3 dock hover-edge morph flicker REPRODUCED and root-caused on live :5199 /dock/overview (the dock-capture collapsible dock). The flash is NOT a rapid expand-collapse-expand state-machine thrash (buffered collapseDelay masks that — 0 fast 3-flip oscillations across park/jitter/sweep vectors). It is a ONE-to-TWO-frame GEOMETRIC SCALE POP at collapse-onset: the `.collapsed` class flips on synchronously while the box is still painted at EXPANDED width for the morph's leading frames (the --dock-morph-t re-base/measure defer), and the UNGUARDED `.glass-dock.collapsed:hover { scale: 1.1 }` rule (morph.css:232-237) multiplies the still-535px box instead of the intended 54px collapsed circle — painting a ±24-34px right-edge jump (frame trace: t=4149 right 924.5→948.8 +24.3px held 2 frames, t=4194.8 →914.7 −34.1px). Because the pointer is still inside the box (hover stays true) and the listeners are bound to the morphing root with NO hysteresis, the moving edge re-crosses the stationary pointer — the classic FLIP hover-thrash substrate. Shell docks (BottomDock/SidebarDock) are always-expanded and short-circuit the hover handlers, so they are NOT on this seam. Fix space proposed below; NO implementation per tranche-dev mandate.

- **C2-1** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] → AZ dock-morph wave (R3-3) — R3-3 flicker REPRODUCED live: at collapse-onset the painted right edge of a hovered expanded dock pops +24.3px (held ~43ms / 2 frames) then snaps −34.1px the next frame — the visible flash. Frame trace: t=4149.1 width=535.7 scale=1.1 right=948.8 (collapsed class on, --dock-morph-t still 0), t=4194.8 width=467.3 scale=1 right=914.7.
  - ev: docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/C2-morph-flicker-trace.json (561 frames); docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/C2-morph-flicker-EVIDENCE.md (frame table)
- **C2-2** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] — ROOT CAUSE: `.glass-dock.collapsed:hover { scale: var(--dock-collapsed-hover-scale) }` is UNGUARDED by `:not([data-morphing])`. The `.collapsed` class flips on synchronously when collapse begins, but the box is still at expanded width for the morph's leading frames (the onSwap pin/measure defer re-bases --dock-morph-t from 0 over the first rAF). The hover scale 1.1 — designed for the resting 54px pill — multiplies the still-535px box, producing the geometric pop.

There is NO pointer-rect hysteresis, NO frozen sentinel rect during morph, and NO pointer-capture. onMouseEnter is INSTANT (collapsed→hover→expand same tick); onMouseLeave is timer-buffered by collapseDelay (600ms on dock-capture, 2500ms default).

## C4-hover-register

R3-6 — the iOS-glassy hover/click register at the ROOT. INVENTORY: the four-state interactive chain is split across (a) the ROOT library tokens, which are ALREADY glass-first and red-free, and (b) the consumer (demo) preset, which is where ALL the warm-red the user objects to actually lives. Live capture (light+dark) proves: the HOVER register is correct — bg lifts to a glass tier (--glass-bg-resting), scale --scale-hover-dock=1.1, color stays warm-ink; NO red on hover of a non-selected control. The SELECTED register is where red enters: the bottom-dock active tab paints its glyph NCSU-red (oklch(0.579 0.201 30.4)=--viz-fourier) and the sidebar rail active item paints its glyph red + a solid-red leading-edge ::before bar. The RED IS NOT A LIBRARY DEFAULT — the library default for both selected accents is --primary (warm-ink hsl(24 10% 10%)); the demo overrides --demo-nav-accent→--viz-fourier and re-points --dock-rail-active-accent + --dock-active-color to it (demo/layout/dock-nav.css:21,58,103). TENSION (for synthesis): the warm-red SELECTED register was a PRIOR USER DECISION (the NCSU brand wordmark + viz-basis Fourier-red identity; CLAUDE.md 'presets in consumers'), and the user now says 'I do not like the red' on hover/click. R3-6 says fix it 'at the root, within our icons and buttons' — so the correct AZ resolution is NOT just retinting the demo preset (which would leave the library default --primary register intact and re-introduce red on the next consumer), but redefining the ROOT selected register to the iOS-glassy luminance-lift model AND retiring the demo's red glyph/accent override. The iOS 26/27 + macOS Control-Center register: SELECTED = a translucent material-tint plate (a white/luminance lift OVER the blurred substrate), NOT a saturated brand glyph + NOT a solid accent bar; HOVER = a lighter same-material lift; PRESSED = a brief darken + scale-down (~0.96). The library's --dock-control-active-bg=--glass-bg-floating is ALREADY the correct iOS selected-plate; the defect is the RED GLYPH + RED ACCENT BAR riding ON TOP of it. DRAFT redesign below. NO source edits made (development-only).

- **C4-INV-1-active-bg-already-glass** [VERIFIED S3] — ROOT dock selected-plate is ALREADY glass-correct (NOT a saturated brand fill). --dock-control-active-bg defaults to var(--glass-bg-floating) (the 80% glass tier, a measurable step above the --glass-bg-resting hover tier), and --dock-active-bg re-points to it. This is the iOS 'selected reads as glass' model already shipped (AX.W45-TUNE / AX.W45 DK2). The earlier opaque --muted plate was already retired. So the active SURFACE is not the red the user means.

## C5-adaptive-legibility

R3-7 audit: the W55 --glass-backdrop bucket is consumer-DECLARATIVE (default `dark`, zero-delta), read by `@container style(--glass-backdrop: light)` blocks. Mechanism file:line-confirmed. The headline defect is REAL and LIVE-PROVEN: the dock over light content does NOT darken automatically. The dock sets `--glass-backdrop: light` on its OWN root (shell.css:73) but style queries never self-match, and — unlike the overlay band (.glass-floating/.glass-overlay), which has a genuine unconditional self-engage `:where()` rule at ladder.css:169 — the dock has NO self-engage rule. The only dock tint re-point is the ancestor-querying `@container` block (morph.css:295), which never fires because no demo route (and, by design, no consumer-set default) sets the bucket on a dock ancestor. Live π-readback on 6 dock routes: every dock reports `--glass-backdrop: light` but `--glass-tint-strength: 0%`, dock plate paints L=0.88 over a page L=0.89 (ΔL≈0.01, silhouette invisible). The shell.css:67-73 comment CLAIMING a self-engage rule "darkens the dock's OWN plate unconditionally" is factually false — no such rule exists. The source gate + π arm both pass (the π arm injects the bucket on a synthetic ancestor; the gate only structure-parses the block's presence) — the exact "green gate over a still-broken render" cardinal-lesson gap the gate header warns about. SECONDARY: even where the overlay band DOES self-engage (paper-glass cards, tint=18%), the 18% AA floor was calibrated against `--foreground` ink (~11:1) but real body text widely uses `--muted-foreground`, which over the darkened plate reads 3.42:1, FAILING 4.5:1 — the floor is under-calibrated for the muted ink tier. The `--glass-backdrop-luma` token is minted empty + reserved (tokens/glass.css:248), referenced by NO cascade consumer and NO observer — the hook the AZ automatic facility drives. AZ design space for the sampled-luminance observer is laid out below: feasibility GOOD, perf budget bounded, declarative bucket as floor.

- **C5-1** [VERIFIED] — W55 SHIPPED MECHANISM (state-of-W55): the --glass-backdrop bucket is a consumer-DECLARATIVE inherited ident (default `dark` = zero-delta), NOT an automatic signal. There is no web API to read pixels behind a backdrop-filter, so a consumer sets `--glass-backdrop: light` on an ancestor it knows is bright. The default tint axis is byte-identical: `--glass-tint-source: var(--card)` + `--glass-tint-strength: 0%`. The bucket is read by `@container style(--glass-backdrop: light)` blocks that lift `--glass-tint-strength` to `--glass-tint-strength-aa` (18%) and re-point `--glass-tint-source` to `--glass-tint-ink` (= --foreground), mixing the rung toward ink via the EXISTING color-mix(in oklab) seam (zero new compositing seam).
  - ev: src/styles/tokens/glass.css:208-250 (the tint+bucket tokens), src/styles/glass/ladder.css:134-145 (the rung @container block), docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/C5-asymmetry.json
- **C5-2** [OPEN-DEFECT S1] → W55-adaptive-glass / dock-self-engage — R3-7 HEADLINE DEFECT (S1, user-visible): the dock over light content does NOT darken automatically. The dock sets `--glass-backdrop: light` on its OWN root (shell.css:73) but CSS style queries never self-match, and there is NO unconditional self-engage rule for `.glass-dock` (unlike the overlay band's `:where(.glass-floating, .glass-overlay)` at ladder.css:169). The only dock tint re-point is the ancestor-querying `@container style(--glass-backdrop: light) { .glass-dock {...} }` at morph.css:295, which never fires in-situ because no demo route (and no library default) sets the bucket on a dock ANCESTOR. LIVE-PROVEN: on /dock/overview, /dock/layers, /dock/rail, /compositions/settings, /foundations/colors, /data/metric-cell the dock computes `--glass-backdrop: light` but `--glass-tint-strength: 0%`; the dock plate paints near-white L=0.88 over a page L=0.89 (ΔL≈0.01 — the silhouette is invisible, the G2 collapse).

- **C5-8** [DESIGN-FINDING] → W55-adaptive-glass / automatic-observer — AZ DESIGN SPACE — the AUTOMATIC sampled-luminance observer (the facility W55 reserved). The --glass-backdrop-luma token is minted EMPTY at tokens/glass.css:248, read by NO cascade consumer and set by NO observer (grep-confirmed) — it is the pure hook. DESIGN: a `useGlassBackdropLuminance` composable (demo-private until a 2nd consumer, per the no-overfitting bar) that, per registered glass surface, (1) snapshots the region directly behind the element — NOT via reading backdrop-filter output (impossible) but via a cheap downsampled-canvas read of the page background layer OR `document.elementsFromPoint` walking the stack below the surface rect to composite the painted backdrop; (2) computes WCAG relative luminance; (3) writes `--glass-backdrop-luma` (0..1) AND derives the discrete `--glass-backdrop: light|dark` bucket (threshold ~0.6) on the surface; (4) rAF-THROTTLED + IntersectionObserver-gated (only sample visible surfaces) + ResizeObserver/scroll-coalesced (sample on layout-settle, not every frame). The declarative bucket stays the FLOOR (the observer refines, never the sole path) — matching the contrast-color() progressive-enhancement doctrine.
  - ev: src/styles/tokens/glass.css:226-232 (the luma token spec: 'a heavier JS observer sets when the declarative bucket is too coarse … the OBSERVER stays demo-private until a 2nd consumer') + :248 (minted empty) | grep: --glass-backdrop-luma has zero cascade-read consumers
- **C5-9** [DESIGN-FINDING] → W55-adaptive-glass / automatic-observer — AZ DESIGN SPACE — observer FEASIBILITY + PERF BUDGET. FEASIBILITY: the only sound backdrop signal without a behind-the-backdrop-filter pixel API is (a) elementsFromPoint stack-walk composite (cheap, synchronous, no canvas — preferred for static page backgrounds) for the common case of a glass surface over a solid/gradient page; (b) a downsampled offscreen-canvas drawImage of a known background-layer element (the aurora/grid/paper substrate canvas) for animated WebGL/Canvas backdrops — sample at a 16x16 or 32x32 downscale, read getImageData once per settle. BUDGET: dock + ~3-8 content cards per route = <12 sampled surfaces; IntersectionObserver gates to visible ones; rAF-throttle to ≤4 Hz (250ms) on scroll/resize-settle, IDLE between. A 16x16 getImageData is ~1KB readback, sub-ms. Total budget target <2ms/settle, ZERO steady-state cost (no per-frame sampling). The animated-backdrop case (aurora behind a dock) is the only one needing periodic re-sample; gate it behind a per-surface `data-glass-sample="live"` opt-in so static surfaces sample ONCE on mount + layout-settle. Risk: cross-origin canvas taint (none here — same-origin), and the elementsFromPoint walk missing a backdrop-filter sibling (acceptable — the declarative bucket floor covers it).
  - ev: Design synthesis grounded in: src/composables/dom/useResizeObserver.ts + src/composables/motion/useIntersectionPause.ts + useRAFLoop (the existing throttle/gate substrates the observer would compose) | the WebGL substrate's offscreen-pause precedent (CLAUDE.md §WebGL substrate offscreen-pause) for the visible-only gating model
- **C5-10** [DESIGN-FINDING] → W55-adaptive-glass — AZ DESIGN RECOMMENDATION (synthesis): the elegant gestalt fix is TWO layered moves, not one. MOVE 1 (cheap, ships now, no observer): add the unconditional self-engage `:where()` default to the over-light-common surfaces — the dock (.glass-dock) AND the plain content tiers (.glass-card/.glass-resting) — mirroring the overlay band's ladder.css:169, so the library's default register (glass-first MAXIMAL per W54) is legible over light WITHOUT consumer opt-in. Recalibrate --glass-tint-strength-aa so the MUTED ink tier (not just --foreground) clears 4.5:1 (C5-5). This closes R3-7 for the static case with zero JS. MOVE 2 (the reserved automatic facility): the rAF-throttled sampled-luminance observer (C5-8/C5-9) refines the discrete bucket into the continuous --glass-backdrop-luma for the ANIMATED-backdrop case (dock over live aurora) where a static `light`/`dark` bucket is too coarse — progressive enhancement over the Move-1 floor. The π gate must be extended to read the dock + content tiers IN-SITU (no injected ancestor bucket) so the C5-4 blind spot cannot recur.
  - ev: Synthesis of C5-2 through C5-9 | the self-engage pattern: src/styles/glass/ladder.css:169-172 | the floor recalibration: src/styles/tokens/glass.css:250 | the in-situ gate gap: tests-visual/adaptive-glass.spec.ts:367-431 (G1 omits dock)

## C6-blob

Live-audited /substrates/blob at :5199 (headless chromium, DPR 2, --use-angle=metal). ROOT-CAUSED R3-9's two complaints and enumerated R3-10's studio refinement space. KEY FINDING: the GL GooBlob is NOT the pixelation source — its DPR handling is correct (backing-store 820×820 for a 410×410 CSS canvas = 2.002 px/CSS px at DPR 2, see c6-blob-01-full diag JSON). The "main blobs at the top are pixelated" are the SECTION-1 WatercolorDot swatches (CSS-px-resolution SVG feTurbulence/feDisplacementMap edge), which is a DIFFERENT element than the user assumes (they read the static SVG dots as the living GL creatures). The "no satellites metaballing in/out" is a real GEOMETRY defect: orbitRadius (0.17) < bodyRadius (0.22), so GL satellites orbit INSIDE the body silhouette and never separate into a visible distinct droplet — they merge entirely under the body skin (the AX.W15 four-side-containment re-solve killed the orbit-then-merge show). W-BLOB-GLASS (uBackdrop Snell refraction + squircle bevel, G-PERF/G-BROWSER) is a fully-specced CONDITIONAL-greenlit wave that folds into this family. Captures under docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/c6-blob-*.png. NO source edits, NO git mutations; temp capture scripts run from /tmp and removed.

The studio bead renders crisp and smooth (c6-blob-09-studio-bead-zoom.png). The README promise 'crisp at any zoom or device-pixel ratio' (README.md:66) HOLDS for the SDF metaball.

The keyframes.js repo (present at ~/Programming/keyframes.js) is the design-language source: EASING_GROUPS (10 families/~35 curves), EasingEditor (dropdown + editable bezier canvas + readout/copy on a glass Card surface="cartoon"), scene-machine animation demos (Cube/Amiga/Square/Easing/Spring/Sequence/Path), ppmycota-purple = hsl(248 88% 71%); glass-ui's natural one-color-event accent is --section-color-1 oklch(0.502 0.165 305.9) purple. glass-ui already ships all chassis primitives needed (Configurator, SegmentedTabs, LabeledSlider, Slider). ## C8-logo-morph-fourier

All three R3 items verified live. (a) R3-12 CONFIRMED: the shell rail (demo/layout/SidebarDock.vue) stacks TWO Foundations affordances with no divider — the ℱ wordmark home control (#persistent slot, lines 99-121, →"/" → router.ts:14 firstStoryPath() → /foundations/intro) sits directly above the first primary-category DockIconButton, which renders the Compass icon (manifest.ts:109 icon: Compass = the Foundations category). That ℱ-then-Compass adjacency IS the "duplicated compass" (ground: C8-rail-home-region.png). The fix is to make ℱ BE the Foundations entry (drop the redundant Compass row), demarcate it with a DockSeparator, and size it slightly larger. (b) R3-15 CONFIRMED + MEASURED: the ℱ link is a bare 40x40 rounded-full with NO hover-pill bg/shadow of its own (C8-hover-pill-probe.mjs: bg rgba(0,0,0,0), boxShadow none — the "hover/shadow area" is the rounded-full circle itself). Live red-ink scan (C8-fourier-f-livescan.mjs) measures the glyph ink-mass center at dx=+2.38px / dy=+3.25px off the circle's geometric center — the italic script-F slant + ink asymmetry; fix is optical centering (a transform nudge), not geometric flex-center. (c) R3-13 morph showcase: inventoried all pieces and drafted 3 candidate architectures. CRITICAL FINDING — a vertical->horizontal dock morph is a TOPOLOGY change (flex column->row + simultaneous two-axis size change), and AX.W42 fold 7 explicitly records that the web CANNOT continuously interpolate mismatched-topology silhouettes. The current dock morph engine (dockMorphContext.ts) animates ONE size axis only off a single 0->1 scalar; it has no two-axis or orientation-flip capability. The feasible path is the metaball-bridge crossfade (Arch B) — keyframes-driven, deterministic, bidirectional, no topology-jump-cut.

- **C8-R3-12-a** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] → AZ dock-nav-pattern / logo wave (R3-12) — The 'f' logo / duplicated-compass defect: the shell rail's #persistent slot renders the ℱ (U+2131 script-F) wordmark as a RouterLink to '/' (home), and '/' redirects to firstStoryPath() = /foundations/intro. Directly below it, with NO divider, the first primary-category DockIconButton renders the Compass icon — and Compass IS the Foundations category icon (manifest.ts:109). So the rail stacks TWO Foundations affordances (ℱ→foundations/intro AND Compass→foundations category) at the top. This is the user's 'duplicated compass'. The R3-12 fix: make the ℱ logo BE the Foundations entry (remove the redundant Compass DockIconButton for foundations), demarcate it with a DockSeparator below it, and size it slightly larger than the category glyphs.

- **C8-R3-15-b** [OPEN-DEFECT S3] → AZ dock-nav-pattern / logo wave (R3-15) — The fourier-F is NOT centered in its hover/shadow area, MEASURED. The ℱ home control is a bare 40x40 rounded-full RouterLink with focus-ring + tap-squish but NO hover-pill background or box-shadow of its OWN (the link element paints transparent bg, boxShadow:none — the rounded-full circle IS the hover/shadow shape; the only pill behind is the glass-dock rail plate). A live red-ink pixel scan over the exact 40x40 link rect measures the ℱ ink-mass center at dx=+2.38px (right) and dy=+3.25px (below) of the circle's geometric center. The cause: the glyph is italic script-F (font-variation-settings WONK 1) — the italic slant + the script-F's intrinsic ink asymmetry push the painted ink off the advance-box center. The fix is OPTICAL centering (a small translate nudge of roughly -2.4px x / -3.3px y, or a baseline/leading adjustment), NOT geometric flex-centering (which is already in place via items-center justify-center).

- **C8-R3-13-engine** [DESIGN-FINDING] → AZ R3-13 morph-showcase wave — PIECE 1 (the dock FLIP morph engine): ONE SpringProgress (keyframes.js LIGHT-barrel) per dock, writing a single normalized 0->1 scalar --dock-morph-t once/frame to the .glass-dock root (an inheriting @property <number>). Each morph target carries its own --dock-morph-from/--dock-morph-to px span, interpolated as from+(to-from)*t. Interruptible (re-bases the live spring from current velocity on a mid-flight re-toggle; DOCK_SPRING response 0.32 / ζ 0.7). CRITICAL LIMITATION for R3-13: this engine animates exactly ONE size axis (dimOf(axis) = width for horizontal, height for vertical, derived from the FIXED orientation prop). It has NO machinery to (a) flip orientation, (b) flip flex-direction column<->row, or (c) animate BOTH dimensions simultaneously (a vertical->horizontal morph must shrink height WHILE growing width). Live trace confirms a one-axis width morph 34->535px on one scalar with 0 oscillations.

- **C8-R3-13-goo** [DESIGN-FINDING] → AZ R3-13 morph-showcase wave — PIECE 2 (the goo-blob metaball substrate): a WebGL2 metaball droplet on the shared useWebGLCanvas substrate. The gooey merge is done ENTIRELY in the fragment shader via signed-distance smin (IQ 2024 normalized quadratic + circular variants, config-gated on uMerge); the silhouette is up-to-4 satellites smin-merged with FBM-displaced watercolor edges. It ALREADY ships volume-preserving anisotropic squish (sa = 1+tanh(speed*1.6)*uStretch, exactly 1/sa perpendicular — metaball.frag.ts:194-215) and signed pointer-lean flex (metaball.frag.ts:318-326). CRITICAL CONSTRAINT for R3-13: the goo-blob is a CONTAINED creature painted on its OWN oversized canvas (1.6x the wrapper), NOT a layout substrate — it cannot host dock children or be the dock's actual box. It can only be a DECORATIVE bridge painted BEHIND/AROUND a real morphing DOM dock (the metaball-teardrop look), never the structural dock itself. There is NO CSS SVG goo filter (feGaussianBlur+feColorMatrix threshold) anywhere in src/styles — the only SVG filters are paper-grain feTurbulence; the gooeyness is shader-only.

CRITICAL for R3-13: W42 fold 7 (§19.11 dock-self-reshape) explicitly records the honest web-platform answer — a CSS engine CANNOT continuously interpolate mismatched-topology clip-path silhouettes (arbitrary shape-to-shape morphs jump-cut), so it NARROWS reshape to (a) continuous superellipse-k (same topology) and (b) discrete custom-preset cross-fades via the 3-state lifecycle. A vertical<->horizontal dock orientation flip is a topology+two-axis change, which falls under the explicit NO-GO 'arbitrary free-form silhouette' class for a CONTINUOUS clip-path morph. W-LIQUID (the Siri amorphous-flex facility, OPEN) is ~85% divinable from shipped code; the genuine research residue is only Siri-orb reference bands. Neither booked wave currently specs an orientation-flip morph — R3-13 is net-new and must respect the topology-morph platform limit.

- **C8-R3-13-archA** [DESIGN-FINDING] → AZ R3-13 morph-showcase wave — ARCHITECTURE A — Two-axis single-scalar reshape on the existing engine (border-radius/squircle continuous, NO orientation flip). Generalize dockMorphContext to drive TWO size axes off the ONE --dock-morph-t scalar (add a second --dock-morph-from-cross/--dock-morph-to-cross span so width AND height interpolate together) plus a continuous --superellipse-k (or corner-shape: superellipse(k)) calc'd off the scalar so corners relax during travel. This gives a smooth liquid box-reshape WITHOUT a flex-direction flip — the children stay in the same flow, only the box + corner squircle morph. PERF: cheap (1 spring, calc-only CSS, composited @property, no shader, no per-frame layout if FLIP-measured once). VERDICT: deterministic + bidirectional + keyframes-driven, but does NOT achieve the vertical->horizontal RE-FLOW the user asked for — it's a reshape, not an orientation flip. Best as a smaller-scope 'liquid dock reshape' demo or a building block, not the full R3-13.

- **C8-R3-13-archB** [DESIGN-FINDING] → AZ R3-13 morph-showcase wave (recommended) — ARCHITECTURE B — Metaball-bridge crossfade (RECOMMENDED; achieves the amorphous teardrop vertical->horizontal). Render BOTH a vertical and a horizontal real DOM dock; during the morph, drive the existing one-axis spring on EACH (vertical collapses its height->0 while horizontal grows its width 0->full, both off ONE shared --dock-morph-t for determinism), and paint a goo-blob / SVG-goo (feGaussianBlur+feColorMatrix threshold) metaball bridge BEHIND them that smin-merges the two shrinking/growing plates into one amorphous teardrop that flows from vertical to horizontal. The DOM docks crossfade (opacity off the same scalar) under the goo veneer so the topology jump-cut (column->row reflow) happens at the visually-hidden midpoint where the goo blob fully occludes it. PERF: one spring + one WebGL2 metaball pass (already offscreen-pause + PRM-aware) OR a cheaper CSS SVG-goo filter on Chromium; bidirectional + deterministic because it's one 0->1 scalar both directions. This is the faithful liquid-glass metaball-teardrop the user named, and it sidesteps the topology-morph platform limit by hiding the reflow inside the goo merge. NOTE: no CSS SVG goo filter exists yet — it would be net-new (a feGaussianBlur+feColorMatrix threshold filter, the classic gooey-CSS trick).

- **C8-R3-13-archC** [DESIGN-FINDING] → AZ R3-13 morph-showcase wave — ARCHITECTURE C — Pure WebGL2 metaball dock (full liquid, highest fidelity, highest cost). Render the dock's silhouette ENTIRELY as a goo-blob metaball field (the dock plate IS a single large metaball, the icons are HTML overlaid + positioned via JS off the same morph scalar), and morph the metaball's aspect/orientation by interpolating its SDF parameters (the body becomes a tall capsule -> a wide capsule, smin-blended through an amorphous teardrop midpoint). The shader ALREADY does volume-preserving anisotropic squish (sa/1.0/sa) so a tall->wide aspect morph is a natural SDF parameter sweep — no topology jump-cut because the SDF is continuous. PERF: most expensive (a live WebGL2 pass for the chrome itself, not a decorative bridge; FBM-per-pixel + the OKLCh round-trip), and the icon-overlay positioning must be hand-synced to the SDF — fragile, and the dock chrome would no longer be real DOM glass (loses backdrop-filter, a11y of the plate). VERDICT: maximally faithful to 'amorphous metaball', but violates the contained-creature constraint (goo-blob can't host children) and the glass-first canon (real DOM glass); over-engineered for a showcase. Reach for B over C.

## D1-hierarchy

Live walk of all 12 demo categories on :5199. The storybook's PAGE-LEVEL chrome is consistent and clean (every page: eyebrow CATEGORY·STORY + a `text-heading` h1 + subtitle, via StoryPage.vue:37-41). The hierarchy breaks DOWN one level: section headings (h2) are hand-picked per-page with NO canonical token, ranging 14px→25.9px across pages, so the eye often lands on demo content before the labels that organize it. The single highest-leverage fix is a canonical section-heading rung applied uniformly (the `text-subheading` token / `<StorySection>` chassis already exist but ~several pages bypass them for `text-sm font-semibold text-muted-foreground`). Secondary: duplicate/competing titles on data-table + intro, the switcher-rail's inverted hierarchy (R3-1, partially-addressed: icons now paint but are faint/cramped), and dark slider-range bars dominating the soft Settings page. Captures under docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D1-*.png.

- **D1-1** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → design-hierarchy structuring — SYSTEMATIC ROOT: no canonical section-heading rung. Section h2s are hand-rolled per page with three incompatible patterns — `text-subheading` (20.4px/600, the canonical token) on dock/layers + containers/sheet; `text-sm font-semibold text-muted-foreground` (14px/600, BELOW body text, reads as a caption not a heading) on dock/overview + navigation/tabs; `text-heading` (25.9px/700, identical to the page title) on display/section + data-table. The canonical ladder is text-heading(700/25.9px page-title) → text-subheading(600/20.4px section) per typography.css:271-285, and the demo ships a `<StorySection>` chassis CLAUDE.md says stories should use — but several pages bypass both, so section structure is muddy library-wide.

## D2-typography

The house OWNS a magnificent audacious type ladder (golden-ratio √φ scale, display-1..5 + mega/hero/audacious tiers peaking at 352px, text-hero poster register, text-pane-title, fourier-F ornament — typography.css:99-426) but the demo STARVES it. The per-page title chassis caps EVERY story at text-heading (live-measured 25.888px, φ) regardless of whether the page is a hero or content (StoryPage.vue:41). 8 of 12 story categories (forms 0/11, display 0/11, containers 0/13, data 0/13, feedback 0/7, dock 0/3, navigation 0/2, substrates 0/5 — ~75 pages) have ZERO display moments; their section heads cap at text-subheading (20.352px, √φ). The two top audacious tiers (--type-display-mega φ^9/2 / --type-display-audacious φ^11/2 — 177px/352px peaks) have ZERO live consumers — dead minted substrate. Type SINGS only where a hero body is hand-authored (intro h1 at 86px display-4, hero composition h2 at 86px) — never chassis-driven. The user's bar ("large and audacious typography SUFFUSED") is unmet: the ladder exists, it's just not WIRED. Uplift = drive display register through the chassis (hero-flagged pages → text-display-3/4 title) + give the 8 flat categories at least a display-register section-head register + activate the dead mega/audacious tiers on the metric/number/hero surfaces.

- **D2-1** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] → type-suffusion / page-chassis display-register — The per-page title chassis caps EVERY story at text-heading (φ ~26px) — the global flat-type root cause. StoryPage.vue:41 renders the page <h1> as class="text-heading" for ALL pages including the 9 hero-flagged ones; live-measured 25.888px/weight-700 on intro, typography, hero, dialog, buttons, inputs alike. The hero variant never upgrades the chassis title. A design system that ships poster-type wears a documentation-sized page heading everywhere.

The Dialog/Buttons/Inputs pages read as plain docs, not as a design system.

## D3-color-pops

FRONTEND-DESIGN color audit grounded against the live demo (:5199, 12 fullPage captures under docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D3-*.png). The MODEL is verified: the icons-page "Pops" row (icons.vue:124-164) + empty-states cards (empty-states.vue:130-139) ship the chip recipe `color-mix(in srgb, var(--section-color-N) 25%, transparent)` backplate + full-chroma glyph, ONE color event per surface, chip ≤ icon scale, body ink untinted. The increase-within-proportion map: (1) MIS-SPENT red — the demo dock active register resolves to Fourier-RED oklch(0.579 0.201 30.4) live (the exact R3-6 dislike), set by dock-nav.css:21/58/103; re-point --demo-nav-accent off --viz-fourier to the iOS-glassy register (a glass tier + a cool/neutral glyph tint, NOT red). (2) The whole MOTION category (motion/curve-gallery/springs/typewriter/underline) reads Fourier-red/orange when R3-11 mandates the keyframes ppmycota PURPLE — mint one --motion-accent: var(--viz-legendre) (oklch 0.532 0.180 317.5 = the glass-ui violet twin of ppmycota) and re-point the dots/plots/sample blocks onto it, giving the motion family ONE coherent purple identity. (3) FLAT surfaces that earn a proportioned chip/glyph event: metric-cell (no accent axis — leading icon could tint to its semantic --chart-* viz color), and the section-marker colored-label idiom (settings.vue:78 inline `color: var(--section-color-N)`) is a SHIPPED but ad-hoc pattern worth abstracting + applying to other flat composition pages. Positive idioms (do NOT touch): empty-states/icons chips, settings/timeline section-color markers, notification semantic tones, gate-pattern text-success — all correctly proportioned. The icon GRID and Section type-ladder pages are LEGITIMATELY monochrome (the chips/type are the event; adding color would break proportion).

- **D3-1** [VERIFIED S3] — MODEL VERIFIED — the color-pop chip recipe is the brand's one-color-event grammar: color-mix(in srgb, var(--section-color-N) 25%, transparent) circular backplate under a full-chroma glyph, walked across the 13-stop --section-color ramp; the proportion rule (one color event per surface, chip never exceeds icon scale, body ink never tinted) is documented inline. Shipped on the icons 'Pops' row and the empty-states cards.

## D4-glass-grid-math

SUFFUSION MAP (glass/grid/math design language across the demo's 121 routes). The library SHIPS the full vocabulary — `.section-label` + a 13-stop `--section-color-0..12` ramp (typography.css:455, theme.css:266-278), `paper-grain-overlay`/`paper-texture`/`paper-underpaint` (paper.css, cards.css:10), the blueprint `.story-bg-grid` recipe (story-hero.css:156), the five-rung glass tier ladder, fira-code math + audacious figures — but the DEMO under-spends it. The page background is a declarative per-row `background:` field consumed once by StoryPage.vue:28 -> StoryHero.vue (no page hand-rolls a backdrop). Of 121 routes only ~17 declare ANY background (the heros + grid/paper refs); ~104 wear the flat default — a single `resting`-tier (~0.65α) glass card on a flat warm-white page with ZERO glass-tier read, ZERO paper/grid underlay, ZERO math/section marginalia. The DOMINANT axis is UNDER-spend (thinness on every content page — forms, tables, dialogs, feedback, settings, containers, the composables shelf). OVER-spend is near-absent (no content page stacks competing substrates; the only multi-substrate pages are the substrate demos themselves, intentional). A SECOND, architectural over-RESTRAINT counter: even the pages that DO declare grid/paper read flat, because the grid color is `--foreground 7%` (story-hero.css:15) painted UNDER a 0.65α-opaque `resting` card — the suffusion is declared but suppressed by the opacity stack. The GOLD STANDARD is compositions/math-paper.vue (capture d4-suf-math-paper-grid.png): indigo section-accent rail + `§ 3 · Convergence` mono label + italic fourier S glyph + fira-code math block on a paper-grain article — the content-level idiom that should propagate to the thin pages WITHIN PROPORTION. R3's directive (suffusion: glass, grid, math, large audacious typography, colorful pops) names exactly this; the math-paper/metric-stack pages prove the language works when content composes it. The restraint counter: do NOT add live substrates to content pages (kills legibility + WebGL budget); the lever is the CALM washes (grid/paper) lifted to readable strength + the content-level section-accent/math-marginalia idiom — NOT another aurora.

- **D4-1** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → page-redesign suffusion / W60-class container-layer — THIN SURFACE (canonical): compositions/settings — a page literally ABOUT 'Grain' and 'Paper underpaint' toggle controls renders as flat off-white nested `Card border-2 border-foreground/10` blocks on a flat warm-white page. No declared background (manifest row has no `background:`), no visible paper grain, no grid, no glass-tier read, no math/section accent. The page is monochrome white-on-white; the only color is the bottom-dock warm-red active label. It SHOULD carry the calm paper-grain underlay (its own subject) + a section-accent rail per group, within proportion.
  - ev: docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/d4-thin-settings.png; demo/stories/compositions/settings.vue:84,116,205,244 (nested flat Cards); manifest.ts settings row declares no background
- **D4-2** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → page-redesign suffusion / W60-class container-layer — THIN SURFACE (breadth): the entire content-page population wears the flat default. ~104 of 121 routes declare NO `background` — forms/* (11), display/* (11), containers/* (13), data/{table,data-table,tags-input,avatar,sortable-list,infinite-scroll,timeline*,search,scrolling-text}, feedback/* (7), navigation/tabs, motion/{curve-gallery,countup,reveal,typewriter,underline,animated-digit}, the 22-row composables shelf. Each is a single `resting` glass card (StoryHero cardTier default, ~0.65α) on a flat page. The design language is absent on the bulk of the storybook.
  - ev: demo/stories/manifest.ts (parsed: only ~17 rows carry `background:`); demo/stories/StoryHero.vue:132-135 (cardTier defaults to `resting` off a live substrate); captures d4-thin-forms-inputs.png, d4-thin-data-table.png, d4-thin-feedback-alert.png, d4-thin-dialog.png
- **D4-3** [DESIGN-FINDING S3] → page-redesign suffusion / W60-class container-layer — OVER-RESTRAINT COUNTER (architectural): even the pages that DO declare grid/paper read flat because the suffusion is suppressed by the opacity stack. The blueprint grid is `--story-grid-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--foreground) 7%, transparent)` (story-hero.css:15) painted UNDER a `resting`/`wash`-tier card; on data/metric-cell + data/metric-stack (both declare `grid`) the grid is effectively invisible. The fix is NOT a louder grid everywhere but lifting the declared-background strength toward readable AND/OR dropping the content card to a thinner tier (`quiet`/`wash`) so the calm wash reads — the lever already exists (StoryHero already drops to `wash`/`quiet` over LIVE substrates; extend the same thinning to grid/paper).

## D5-animation-targets

FRONTEND-DESIGN motion audit. HEADLINE: motion is well-engineered at the TOKEN/composable layer (the §6 easing doctrine is real and the button family verifiably honors it — surface legs ride --ease-standard, the scale leg rides --spring-smooth, confirmed live at display/buttons) but is BARELY USED as a design pillar across the storybook. The library ships vReveal, useCountup, [data-scroll-reveal]/.scroll-progress native scroll-driven recipes, pane-swap/fade transitions, and useViewTransition — yet content-entrance usage is ZERO on every audited content page (foundations/intro, display/card, dock/overview, compositions/settings all return 0 data-reveal / 0 data-countup / 0 scroll-reveal), the StoryPage chassis every story uses has NO entrance, and the RouterView has NO page transition (story nav is a hard cut). Within the dedicated /motion category the showcase is strong (Curve Gallery is SOTA, underline/typewriter/animated-digit/reveal all solid) but two stories FORK off the new canonical curve table instead of consuming it (springs.vue hand-rolls damped() and mislabels 'smooth' as critically-damped, contradicting both SPRING_PRESETS and its own sibling Curve Gallery; countup.vue hand-rolls easeOutBack), there is redundancy between /foundations/motion and /motion/curve-gallery, and R3-11's 'keyframes.js ppmycota purple' identity appears nowhere (motion demo paints the warm-red --primary the user dislikes). On the dock: R3-3 edge-flicker has a real root cause (no hysteresis/dwell/geometry guard on the hover-expand → the morphing box edge re-crosses the static cursor and re-fires enter/leave), and R3-13's vertical↔horizontal liquid morph is a genuinely-missing facility (the morph substrate is single-axis by construction — dimOf(axis) morphs WITHIN one axis only, never cross-axis). Captures under docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D5-*.png.

- **D5-1** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → W-MOTION-SUFFUSION (animation-as-pillar) — SHOWCASE GAP (highest leverage): content-entrance motion is entirely dead across the storybook. The StoryPage chassis (demo/stories/StoryPage.vue:33-67) — used by EVERY story — paints its header (eyebrow/title/blurb) and section stack instantly with no entrance. Live probe returns 0 [data-reveal], 0 [data-countup], 0 [data-scroll-reveal], 0 .scroll-progress across foundations/intro, display/card, dock/overview, compositions/settings. The library SHIPS vReveal (built to drive exactly the eyebrow→title→blurb→section cascade) but its own showcase never uses it. Applying the vReveal entrance cascade once in StoryPage/StorySection lights up motion on every page — the single highest-value uplift for 'animation as a design-language pillar'.
  - ev: demo/stories/StoryPage.vue:33-67 (no entrance); docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D5-probe.mjs revealUsage all-zero; src/composables/motion/vReveal.ts (unused by chassis)
- **D5-2** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → W-MOTION-SUFFUSION — SHOWCASE GAP: the RouterView in the demo shell has NO page transition — story-to-story navigation is an instantaneous hard cut. AppShell.vue:129-143 wraps the component in a plain <component :is> with no <Transition>. The library ships pane-swap + fade <Transition> class-sets (transitions.css) and useViewTransition / .gl-list-item view-transitions explicitly for this, all unused by the shell. A demo selling motion as a pillar should glide between stories (the pane-swap register is the natural fit, out-in).
  - ev: demo/layout/AppShell.vue:129-143; src/styles/transitions.css (.pane-swap-* / .fade-* unused by shell); src/composables/motion/useViewTransition.ts
- **D5-3** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] → W-MOTION (curve-canon adoption in stories) — DEMO FORK (R3-11 undermined): demo/stories/motion/springs.vue hand-rolls its own damped(stiffness,damping) spring solver and MISLABELS the registers — its 'smooth' = damped(40,12) blurbed 'Critically damped — no overshoot, calm settle', but the canonical --spring-smooth is ζ=0.86 (SETTLE register, overshoots ~0.5%) and critical damping is GENTLE per SPRING_PRESETS. It contradicts its own sibling story (Curve Gallery correctly shows 'smooth = SETTLE register'). It imports neither MOTION_CURVES nor springPreset — the exact no-fork the new curves.ts/springPresets.ts canon was built to prevent. The Spring Orchestrator should drive off MOTION_CURVES['--spring-*'].js so the showcase teaches the SHIPPED curves.

- **D5-4** [OPEN-DEFECT S3] → W-MOTION (curve-canon adoption in stories) — DEMO FORK: demo/stories/motion/countup.vue hand-rolls a local easeOutBack overshoot (line 13-18) instead of reaching the canonical MOTION_CURVES['--spring-snappy'].js twin (the iOS-control overshoot the curve table exists to publish). Same no-fork-discipline gap as D5-3 — the count-up showcase should demonstrate the shipped curve, not a one-off.

- **D5-5** [GAP S2] → W-MOTION (keyframes-purple register) — R3-11 keyframes-purple identity ABSENT: the motion demo carries no keyframes.js 'ppmycota purple' accent anywhere — springs.vue's animated block paints flat warm-red --primary (the orange square), the foundations/motion play-dots are warm-red beads. The user asked to 'leverage the keyframes.js ppmycota purple' so the motion section reads as the keyframes design language; today it reads as the default warm-red register the user explicitly dislikes (R3-6). Grep for purple/ppmycota across motion stories: zero hits.

- **D5-6** [PRUNE-CANDIDATE S3] → W-MOTION (curve-set consolidation) — REDUNDANCY: /foundations/motion (CSS-half curve tour, demo/stories/foundations/motion.vue) and /motion/curve-gallery (CSS↔JS curve tour) both render the SAME curve set as a translateX dot over a small-multiple plot. The Curve Gallery is strictly richer (JS twin name, (response,ζ) pair, register doc, §6 doctrine legend, plot + dot) — the foundations page is the weaker duplicate (smaller plots, no JS twin, red bead). R3-11 wants the curve-set EXPANDED to all our curves + all keyframes.js timing curves; the consolidation move is to fold the foundations curve table INTO the Curve Gallery's richer presentation and let foundations/motion focus on the <Transition> grammar tour only.
  - ev: demo/stories/foundations/motion.vue:14-50 (curve dot-runner); demo/stories/motion/curve-gallery.vue (richer twin table); docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D5-foundations-motion.png vs D5-motion-curve-gallery.png
- **D5-7** [DESIGN-FINDING S1] → W-DOCK1 (morph edge-flicker) — R3-3 ROOT CAUSE (dock edge-flicker): useDockState has NO hysteresis/dwell/geometry guard on hover-expand. onMouseEnter (useDockState.ts:178-186) immediately flips state→'hover' so the collapsed pill GROWS; collapse is only a collapseDelay timer after onMouseLeave (no geometry recheck of whether the cursor is still inside the post-morph box). When the cursor sits near the collapsed-pill edge, the morphing box boundary re-crosses the static cursor, re-firing enter/leave and oscillating expand↔collapse — the flashing/flickering. Fix is a dwell-debounce on enter + a 'still-inside-post-morph-geometry' recheck (or an intent-delay), not the 2500ms collapse timer that only masks slow exits.
  - ev: src/components/custom/dock/composables/useDockState.ts:130-138 (scheduleCollapse pure timer), :178-207 (onMouseEnter immediate expand, no guard); no getBoundingClientRect/hysteresis in the file
- **D5-8** [GAP S2] → W-LIQUID (R3-13 orientation morph) — R3-13 MISSING FACILITY: the vertical↔horizontal liquid-glass dock morph showcase does not exist AND the substrate cannot do it today. dockMorphContext (dimOf(axis)/morphAxisProp, :155-162) and useLayerTransition (dim computed, :89-95) pick ONE axis from the STATIC orientation and morph WITHIN it (collapse/expand along inline OR block) — there is no cross-axis (width-dock→height-dock) transition path. The dock stories show vertical+horizontal docks side-by-side as STATIC examples (rail.vue:140-141, layers.vue:233) with no animated orientation toggle. R3-13's amorphous metaball-teardrop bidirectional morph is the AY-booked W-LIQUID facility folding into AZ; it needs a new 2D shape-interpolation transition, not the existing 1D size morph.
  - ev: src/components/custom/dock/composables/dockMorphContext.ts:155-162 (single-axis dimOf); useLayerTransition.ts:89-95; demo/stories/dock/rail.vue:130-141 (static side-by-side, no morph)
- **D5-9** [GAP S2] → W-MOTION-SUFFUSION (scroll-driven showcase) — SHOWCASE GAP: the native scroll-driven recipes shipped in scroll-driven.css (.scroll-progress scroll() bar; [data-scroll-reveal] > * view() entry-fade-lift, @supports-gated off-main-thread) are demoed by ZERO stories — only named in the manifest blurb text. These are the SOTA native-CSS motion facilities (the iOS-grade scroll choreography, off-main-thread) with no live demonstration. A motion-as-pillar demo must showcase the scroll-progress rail + view-driven reveal (e.g. on a long content page or the foundations intro).
  - ev: src/styles/scroll-driven.css:32-69 (.scroll-progress + [data-scroll-reveal]); grep data-scroll-reveal|scroll-progress demo/stories/ → only manifest.ts; D5-probe.mjs scrollProgress=0 every route
- **D5-10** [OPEN-DEFECT S3] → W-MOTION (reveal PRM exemplar) — PRM-DISCIPLINE TEACHING GAP: demo/stories/motion/reveal.vue's scoped consumer-CSS (reveal-rise/reveal-fade keyframes) carries NO @media(prefers-reduced-motion:reduce) guard, despite the vReveal docstring stating the consumer owns the PRM discipline. The mandate is 'dead surfaces that should move WITHIN PRM discipline' — the showcase that teaches the directive should model the PRM fence it tells consumers to own (the directive itself is correct; the example CSS under-teaches).

At display/buttons the computed transition splits correctly — surface legs (background-color/border-color/box-shadow/color/opacity) ride --ease-standard cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), and the scale transform leg rides --spring-smooth (the linear() stops byte-match var(--spring-smooth)). ## D6-ui-component-pass

Walked all 41 src/components/ui packages + custom headliners (live captures at :5199 under docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/D6-*.png, source-read where no story). The atom layer is MATURE and glass-cohesion is intact: every overlay-band surface (Dialog/Sheet/Toast/Command/Popover/DropdownMenu) composes glass-floating, the five-tier Card ladder POPs over a gradient backdrop, Button/Badge/Alert CVAs are clean four-state, and the opaque-allowlist atoms (Checkbox/Radio 16px, Separator, Skeleton, Table, Avatar-photo) are correctly opaque. The library <GlassDock> Dock Rail renders the CORRECT glass-tier active register. Distinctiveness is strong where the design-language lands (Timeline colorful node rail, MetricStack/MetricBadge large audacious mono numerics, viz-tinted Avatar fallbacks, Slider spectrum gradient track, InstrumentChassis radar dial). Four D-or-worse surfaces feed the AZ roster, all corroborating R3: (1) the DockLayerGroup switcher rail paints a FILLED grey --surface-tint-8 plate, not the hairline the user asked for [R3-1/R3-2]; (2) the DEMO-SHELL SidebarDock active-category is hardcoded NCSU-red, the register R3-6 wants moved to iOS-glassy (library GlassDock is already correct — the fix is the demo shell + the root active token); (3) the Configurator controls column lacks visual hierarchy/proportion [R3-8]; (4) the foundations wordmark logo links to / not Foundations and has no demarcating divider [R3-12]. No glass-cohesion regressions found in ui/ — the component architecture (shadcn-vue pattern, CVA four-state, token-first) is sound; the gaps are design-register/hierarchy, not structure.

- **D6-1** [OPEN-DEFECT S1] → dock-redesign (vertical+horizontal dock, robust layering, hairline-RAIL facility) — DockLayerGroup switcher rail renders as a FILLED grey plate (.dock-layer-rail background: var(--dock-layer-rail-bg, var(--surface-tint-8))), a chunky panel-in-panel fused to the pill's left edge — NOT the hairline rail the user explicitly requested. Icons are now legible (the DK8/B6 fix landed) but the rail is a solid tint block. This is plate-on-plate visually, the carry-forward R3-1/R3-2 headline defect.

## E1-idiom-adopt

Audited the three idiom sources (iOS 26/27, ~/Programming/keyframes.js demo/, ~/Programming/sci-report src/) against glass-ui at tranche/AY HEAD (3.10.1). HEADLINE: most named idioms are ALREADY adopted — iOS Clear/Tinted glass = --glass-level (W54), iOS dynamic-material-darkening = --glass-backdrop @container + contrast-color() (W55), the full keyframes.js spring/curve/suite vocabulary = motion/springPresets.ts + curves.ts + suite.ts, count-up = useCountup, sci-report HandUnderline = GlassUnderline /underline. Three GENUINE adopt candidates survive: (A) sci-report's figure-slug — a CONTAINED audacious-numeric utility (cap-box line-height + contain:layout + overflow:clip + tabular/slashed-zero) over glass-ui's existing --type-display-audacious, which .text-hero lacks the containment backstop for; (B) surfacing the FULL value.js easing catalogue (bounce/sine/circ/expo/steps/bezier families — already a peer dep) into MOTION_CURVES for the R3-11 motion gallery; (C) a family-grouped curve taxonomy (CURVE_FAMILIES + timingFunctionDescriptions) for the gallery presentation. ppmycota purple is correctly NOT adopted (consumer brand, presets-in-consumer rule). Two discrepancies noted: sci-report consumes a published .text-gilt + handmark InkMark/HandMark that are ABSENT in this tree (the tree has .gold-shimmer in the demo + GlassUnderline only). sci-report is a Python e-rate pipeline with a Vue frontend at usf/web/src; its visual idioms mostly FLOW FROM glass-ui (scroll-driven.css, audacious type, handmark) and are thin consumers, not new transposable R&D. Evidence: docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/E1-idiom-adopt-evidence.md.

- **E1-1** [VERIFIED] — iOS 26/27 Clear/Tinted glass modes are ALREADY adopted as the --glass-level typed @property knob (level=1 hand-tuned, level=0 opaque escape). Not an open idiom.
  - ev: src/styles/glass/ladder.css; src/styles/tokens/glass.css; src/styles/glass/surfaces.css (grep glass-level across src/styles)

## E2-refine-extant

Swept src/components/custom/ + ui/ for refinement candidates within extant components. Headline findings: (1) the Metric* family (MetricBadge/MetricPill/MetricCell/MetricRow) is 4 primitives sharing the identical value+unit+placeholder gestalt with NO shared core — and the divergence hides a latent zero-value DEFECT in MetricBadge (`amount || placeholder` renders a valid 0 as the em-dash); it wants ONE value-display abstraction. (2) The R3-2 dock-taxonomy headline confirmed: "rail" is overloaded 3-4 ways and the `variant` vs `orientation` props are partially-redundant axes (variant=rail forces vertical), so "vertical dock" is expressible two ways — grounded by the demo showing both side-by-side. (3) ConfiguratorRow vs LabeledField are two undifferentiated label+control row primitives wanting either a shared chassis or a documented divergence note. Plus smaller helper-dup candidates (isComponent guard, IconLike type). Several families verified as legitimate divergences (DockSeparator, GlassUnderline, ToggleChip, dock control wrappers). NO implementation — tranche development only. Full evidence at docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/E2-refine-extant-findings.md.

- **E2-1** [DESIGN-FINDING S3] → metric-family-unify — Metric* family (4 primitives) shares the identical value+unit+placeholder display gestalt with NO common core: the primary field is named `amount` in MetricBadge/MetricPill but `value` in MetricCell/MetricRow (same concept, two names), `placeholder:'—'` is redeclared 4 times, and the empty-value coalesce logic diverges across all four. Wants ONE shared value-display abstraction (a useMetricValue/coalesceMetric leaf + a single canonical field name + the one correct empty-check) consumed by the four distinct surface SFCs.
  - ev: src/components/custom/metric-badge/MetricBadge.vue:11,41,131; src/components/custom/metric-cell/MetricCell.vue:67,85,113; src/components/custom/metric-stack/MetricRow.vue:48,81,120; src/components/ui/metric-pill/MetricPill.vue:47; docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/E2-refine-extant-findings.md
- **E2-2** [OPEN-DEFECT S2] → metric-family-unify — Latent zero-value DEFECT in MetricBadge: it uses `amount || placeholder` (lines 131, 144) and `!amount` for the muted-class + color gates (lines 129-130), so a VALID metric of 0 renders the em-dash placeholder, muted, with its color stripped. MetricCell.vue:113 and MetricRow.vue:120 use the correct explicit `=== null || === undefined || === ''` check — the divergence the unify (E2-1) would resolve hides this bug.
  - ev: src/components/custom/metric-badge/MetricBadge.vue:129-131,144 (`amount || placeholder`, `!amount`) vs src/components/custom/metric-cell/MetricCell.vue:113 + src/components/custom/metric-stack/MetricRow.vue:120 (explicit empty check)
- **E2-3** [DESIGN-FINDING S2] → dock-taxonomy-disambiguate — R3-2 dock-taxonomy disambiguation confirmed: 'rail' is overloaded — (1) GlassDock variant='rail' = a vertical icon-rail DOCK, (2) DockLayerGroup showRail = the embedded layer-SWITCHER rail, (3) R3-2's intended hairline-RAIL facility (extended divider beyond the dock — does not exist), (4) timeline/ContinuousRail (unrelated). Additionally the `variant` and `orientation` props are partially-redundant axes: variant='rail'/'instrument-strip' FORCE vertical, so 'vertical dock' is expressible two ways. The demo rail.vue shows `<GlassDock variant='rail'>` and `<GlassDock orientation='vertical'>` side-by-side as distinct surfaces — the exact confusion R3-2 names. First-principles re-disambiguation: ONE dock, an orientation axis, both layering-capable, plus a SEPARATE hairline-rail facility.

## E3-gaps

GAPS census for the 7 R3-implied facilities. VERDICT: of the 7, only ONE is wholly absent (R3-7 automatic backdrop-luma observer — explicitly RESERVED, no JS observer ships). The other 6 are PARTIAL — a substrate exists but the R3 mandate exceeds it: (R3-2) hairline-rail-beyond-dock is BOOKED to AY.W-GOD1, not built; the dock taxonomy is ONE GlassDock+orientation-prop, not a disambiguated V/H pair; (R3-13) the dock morph engine is a SIZE-axis (width/height) interpolator, NOT an orientation-axis (V↔H) liquid/metaball morph, and useLiquidFlex (W-LIQUID) is absent; (R3-11) a curve-gallery + foundations/motion exist but cover glass-ui's ~10 MOTION_CURVES only — the FULL value.js named-curve suite (easeIn/Out/InOut × Quad/Cubic/Sine/Circ/Expo + bounce family, ~18 fns), keyframes ScrollTimeline scroll-facilities, and the ppmycota purple are NOT surfaced; (R3-4) the demo configurator exists as a floating PresetEditor FAB (the thing R3-4 wants REMOVED) — the gear-hosted demo-configurator that folds dark-mode + drops composables/floating views does not exist; (R3-14) docks consume ZERO page/route context — no page-driven contextual layer facility exists. Every claim is file:line grounded; NO implementation done.

- **E3G-1** [GAP S1] → AY.W-GOD1 (GlassDock chrome-slot carve) — the R3-2 hairline-rail-beyond-dock lands with the persistent-rail booking — R3-2 HAIRLINE-RAIL PRIMITIVE — the 'extended dividing line that goes BEYOND the dock with a left/right-most icon controlling context' does NOT exist. The only rail today is the IN-dock switcher rail (DockLayerGroup's reka Tabs <TabsList class='dock-layer-rail'>), which lives INSIDE the clipped --full pane and disappears on collapse. A PERSISTENT rail rendered OUTSIDE the pane is explicitly BOOKED (not built) to AY.W-GOD1. Closest substrates to build on: (a) the CSS hairline tokens already minted — --border-hairline / --hairline-accent / --border-border-soft (tokens/glass.css:159, tokens/color-radius.css:89); (b) the axis-aware DockSeparator primitive (DockSeparator.vue:16-17, vertical hairline on row dock / horizontal rule on column dock); (c) the CSS-only instrument-rail.css bezel-hairline+sibling-divider grammar (no Vue host). A 'RailController' primitive (a DockSeparator that extends past the dock edge + binds a leading/trailing context-switch icon) would have 2+ consumers: the demo SidebarDock + BottomDock (demo/layout/), plus dock/rail.vue + dock/layers.vue stories. API sketch: <DockRail extent='beyond' :position='start|end' v-model:context='...' ><DockIconButton/></DockRail> rendered as a GlassDock chrome slot (the W-GOD1 carve) so it survives collapse.

- **E3G-2** [GAP S2] → AY.W-GOD1 / the dock-taxonomy redesign wave — R3-2 DOCK V/H DISAMBIGUATED PAIR — there is NO VerticalDock/HorizontalDock pair. There is ONE GlassDock.vue carrying an orientation?: 'horizontal'|'vertical' prop (default 'horizontal'), plus a discriminated variant union 'dock'|'rail'|'instrument-strip'. Only variant='dock' (horizontal) collapses↔expands; 'rail'/'instrument-strip' are vertical-ALWAYS-EXPANDED (no collapse machinery — the booking note confirms 'a vertical rail dock has no collapse machinery at all'). R3 wants BOTH a vertical AND a horizontal dock that BOTH carry the layering system + BOTH 'morph, shrink, animate'. The substrate is the existing axis-aware machinery: useDockShellProps (variant/shape/orientation resolution), dockMorphContext (dim-keyed width/height), useLayerTransition (axis-aware FLIP). Disambiguation is a NAMING/taxonomy redesign over this substrate, not new compositing. Consumers: SidebarDock (vertical) + BottomDock (horizontal) in demo/layout/ + 3 dock stories. API sketch (no-impl): keep ONE GlassDock, give variant='rail'/instrument-strip a collapse path so the vertical dock can morph/shrink too — OR (R3's 'disambiguate the names') a thin <VerticalDock>/<HorizontalDock> pair over the shared shell.

- **E3G-3** [GAP S2] → AY.W-LIQUID (useLiquidFlex) + a dock-orientation-morph wave — R3-13 LIQUID METABALL MORPH ENGINE (V-dock liquid/amorphous/metaball-teardrop morph to H-dock) — ABSENT. The dock morph engine (dockMorphContext.ts + useDockMorphWindow.ts) interpolates ONE scalar --dock-morph-t over a SIZE axis only: dimOf(axis) maps to width|height, morphAxisProp to inline-size|block-size. It morphs a dock's size within a FIXED orientation; it does NOT transition orientation (V↔H) and has no amorphous/teardrop/metaball geometry. The metaball machinery that DOES exist is wholly separate — GooBlob's useMetaballRenderer (a WebGL2 fragment-shader SDF-blob renderer, not a layout-morph). useLiquidFlex (the W-LIQUID shared amorphous flex+squish primitive named as R3-13's substrate) does NOT exist — grep returns empty. Consumers of the morph showcase: 1 demo story (a new dock/morph-showcase) — borderline on the ≥2 bar, justified as the R3-13 named showcase. API sketch: useLiquidFlex({ from, to, axis }) shared by blob/dock/tabs-indicator (the W-LIQUID 3-name reconcile) + a useDockOrientationMorph layered on --dock-morph-t.

## F2-verify-defects

Adversarially re-reproduced 5 R3 defects on :5199 with my own Playwright captures + measurements under ground/ (prefix F2-). VERDICTS: R3-1 (dock switcher rail) REPRODUCED — rail SVG icons render at a 4px-wide squished sliver. R3-7 (dock-over-light legibility) REPRODUCED with exact root cause — the W55 @container self-engage is a no-op so the dock tint stays 0%. R3-15 (fourier-ℱ centering) REPRODUCED as an OPTICAL defect — metric-box-centered but the ornamental italic ink sits high-right. R3-9 (blob "pixelated/low-res") PARTIALLY REFUTED — WebGL is full-DPR and swatches are smooth at 3x; only the "no satellites/metaballing" half holds. R3-3 (morph edge flicker) NOT REPRODUCED — real-mouse edge probe + the prior C2 trace both show zero width oscillation, only a sub-perceptual spring overshoot (mt 1.046). Harness note: /dock/overview blocks page.evaluate/screenshot in headless unless launched with swiftshader GPU args; full-page screenshots on live-render routes time out (use clip + element shots, or park first). Loud REFUTE flags raised on R3-9 pixelation and R3-3 flicker — a wave grounded on those literal framings would be partial waste.

- **F2-R3-1** [VERIFIED S1] → dock switcher-rail / R3-1 dock-layers — Dock-layers switcher rail icons are broken/illegible — REPRODUCED. The .dock-layer-tab SVG icons render at 4px wide x 16px tall (a squished vertical sliver) inside 28x28 tabs, with viewBox '0 0 24 24' and the size-4 (16px) class set but the COMPUTED width collapsed to 4px. Icons ARE present (component resolves; NOT a first-letter fallback) but width-deformed to faint marks. The rail plate is an 8%-alpha dark tint (color(srgb 0.11 0.098 0.09 / 0.08)), backdrop-filter none.
  - ev: docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/F2-r3-1-rail-tight-zoom.png (three squished glyphs); F2-r3-1-switcher-rail-zoom.png; live measure svgCssW:4px svgCssH:16px viewBox 0 0 24 24 via F2-r3-1-zoom2.mjs; src/components/custom/dock/DockLayerGroup.vue:246 (icon class=size-4); src/styles/dock/layer-group.css:148-160 (.dock-layer-tab 28x28 inline-flex, no svg width/shrink rule)
- **F2-R3-7** [VERIFIED S1] → W55 adaptive-glass dock-over-light / R3-7 — Dock over light backgrounds is unreadable — REPRODUCED with exact root cause. .glass-dock self-declares --glass-backdrop:light (shell.css:73) but the W55 @container style(--glass-backdrop:light) block (morph.css:295) targets .glass-dock — and @container style() queries a CONTAINER ANCESTOR's computed style, not the element's OWN declaration, so the self-engage is a no-op. Live probe: every dock shows glassBackdrop:'light' yet tintStrength:'0%' (never lifts to the 18% --glass-tint-strength-aa floor); dockBg stays oklab(0.9858.../0.42) — a 98.6%-luminance ~transparent near-white plate (ctrl-text vs dock-plate contrast only 1.2:1). FORCING --glass-backdrop:light on document.body lifts tintStrength to 18% (confirms the fix path and the substitution-vs-inheritance trap). The text reads 17:1 vs pure white only incidentally; over a busy/colored bright backdrop there is no contrasting plate.
  - ev: docs/tranches/AZ/audit/ground/F2-r3-7-probe.json (tintStrength 0% on all docks despite glassBackdrop light; forced-body-bucket -> 18%); corroborated by prior-lane C5-dock-probe.json (dockPlateLum 0.884 ~= pageBehindLum 0.889, tintStrength 0%); src/styles/dock/shell.css:67-78; src/styles/dock/morph.css:295-301
- **F2-R3-15** [VERIFIED S3] → fourier-ℱ optical-centering / R3-15 — The fourier ℱ wordmark is not centered in its hover/shadow area — REPRODUCED as an OPTICAL (not metric) defect. Range-bbox measurement: glyph center cx 40.99 / cy 45 vs link-box center cx 41 / cy 45 = offsetX -0.01px, offsetY 0.00px — the glyph's ADVANCE box is perfectly centered in the 40x40 rounded-full link. BUT the prior-lane hover capture shows the ornamental italic ℱ (font-variation WONK 1, font-style italic, 1.875rem) renders with its visible INK biased high-and-right within the centered hover rect (more empty space below than above).
